\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\}
, we will use the projection maps
\hat{\pi}_{s^{x}}\colon X_{\Gamma}^{*}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\hat{V}_{s^{x}}}
and

\check{\pi}_{s^{x}}\colon X_{\Gamma}^{*}\to\mathbb{M}\Gamma_{\check{V}_{s^{x}}}
, which project a
string over
X_{\Gamma}
to the symbols belonging to the nodes in
\hat{V}_{s^{x}}

and
\check{V}_{s^{x}}
, respectively, and return their image in

\mathbb{Z}^{\hat{V}_{s^{x}}}
or
\mathbb{M}\Gamma_{\check{V}_{s^{x}}}
, respectively.
Here
\Gamma_{U}
is the subgraph of
\Gamma
induced by a vertex set
U
.

A
run in
(p,s^{x},q)
is a sequence of transitions

(q_{0},w_{1},q_{1})\cdots(q_{m-1},w_{m},q_{m})

in
\mathcal{A}_{s}
such that
q_{0}=p
,
q_{m}=q
, and

\check{\pi}_{s^{x}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})=1
. Let
R\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{A})
be a subset. An

R
-placeholder run in
(p,s^{x},q)
is a sequence

(11)

\sigma_{0}(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})\sigma_{1}\cdots(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})\sigma_{m}

where (i)
p=q_{0}
and
q=p_{m+1}
, (ii) for
i\in[1,m]
,
s_{i}^{x_{i}}
is above
s^{x}
and
(p_{i},s_{i}^{x_{i}},q_{i})\in R
, (iii)
\sigma_{i}\colon q_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{i}$}\vss}}}p_{i+1}
is a run in
\mathcal{A}_{s}
for each
i\in[0,m]

and (iv)
\check{\pi}_{s^{x}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})=1
.
An
almost
R
-placeholder run in
(p,s^{x},q)
is an
R
-placeholder run
where we also allow
s_{i}^{x_{i}}=s^{x}
and only impose that
\check{\pi}_{{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})=1
.

The
effect
of the
R
-placeholder run in
Eq.

11
is

\displaystyle(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})+\cdots

\displaystyle+(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})

\displaystyle+\hat{\pi}_{s^{x}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}.

The set of effects of
R
-placeholder runs in

(p,s^{x},q)
is denoted
E^{R}_{(p,s^{x},q)}\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}
.
The effect of an almost
R
-placeholder runs is defined as

\displaystyle(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})+\cdots

\displaystyle+(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})

\displaystyle+\hat{\pi}_{{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}.

By
E^{\prime R}_{(p,s^{x},q)}
, we denote the set of effects of almost
R
-placeholder runs in
(p,s^{x},q)
.

We now inductively define which placeholders and which placeholder runs are realizable:

•

An
R
-placeholder run in
\tau
is
realizable
if all
\tau^{\prime}\in R
are realizable.

•

A placeholder
\tau
is
realizable
if there exists a realizable placeholder run in
\tau
.

In particular, all ordinary runs in
\tau
are realizable (set
R=\emptyset
).
The set of realizable placeholder runs in
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
is denoted by
U_{\tau}
.
Every realizable placeholder run is of the form
Eq.

11
(even those that are ordinary runs, which just have
m=0
).
Hence, they have a well-defined effect.
The set of effects of realizable placeholder runs in

(p,s^{x},q)
is denoted
E_{(p,s^{x},q)}\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}
.

The notion of a realizable placeholder run achieves the idea that we have mentioned
above: A realizable placeholder run can only use realizable placeholders, for
which we have already established the existence of a run. Clearly, a
placeholder run in
(p,{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)
is just a neutral run in

(p,{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)
. Thus:

Lemma D.1.

There exists a neutral run from
p
to
q
in
\mathcal{A}
if and only if
E_{(p,{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\neq\emptyset
.

Our goal is to describe the sets
E_{\tau}
using grammars. For this, it will be useful
to have a characterization of
E_{\tau}
that describes how to “build up” elements of
E_{\tau}
successively. This is the purpose of the sets
W_{\tau}
, which we define next.
For this, we need to define the subsets
Y_{r^{x}},Z_{r^{x}}

\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}
:

Z_{r^{x}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}\mid\text{$\mathbf{x}(v)=0$ for every $v\in\check{V}_{r^{x}}$}\\
~\text{and $\mathbf{x}(p,s^{y},q)=0$ for every $p,q\in Q$}\\
~\text{and every $s^{y}$ below $r^{x}$ or equal to $r^{x}$}\}

and
Y_{r^{x}}
is the intersection of all
Z_{s^{y}}
where
s^{y}
is below
r^{x}
.
To simplify notation, if
\tau=(p,r^{x},q)
, we also write
Z_{\tau}
for
Z_{r^{x}}
(analogously for
Y_{\tau}
).
Let us inductively define the tuple
(W_{\tau})_{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})}
, where for
W_{(p,s^{x},q)}\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}
. It is the smallest tuple such that

(1)

If
s
is a leaf and
R\subseteq\{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})\mid W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset\}

then
E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq W_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}
.

(2)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
W_{(p,s^{y},q)}\cap Z_{s^{y}}\neq\emptyset
, then
W_{(p,s^{y},q)}\cap Y_{r^{x}}\subseteq W_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.

(3)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
W_{(p,r^{x},q)}\cap Z_{r^{x}}\neq\emptyset
, then
(p,r^{x},q)\in W_{(p,s^{y},q)}
.

(4)

If
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}\in W_{(p,r^{x},q)}
and
\mathbf{v}\in W_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
, then
\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u}\in W_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.

Once the notion of realizable placeholder runs and the sets
W_{\tau}
is
established, it only requires standard arguments that
E_{\tau}
and
W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
agree.

Proposition D.2.

For every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
, we have
W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}=E_{\tau}
.

Proof.

We say that an almost
R
-placeholder run in
\tau
is
realizable

if all
\tau^{\prime}\in R
are realizable.
Let
E^{\prime}_{\tau}
be the set of all effects of an almost placeholder run in
\tau
.
Observe that
E^{\prime}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}=E_{\tau}
.
Hence it remains to show that
W_{\tau}=E^{\prime}_{\tau}
.
Since the sets
W_{\tau}
and
E^{\prime}_{\tau}
are defined inductively
it is to natural to prove both inclusions of
W_{\tau}=E^{\prime}_{\tau}

by inductions on the number of rules needed to witness that an element belongs to the sets.

First we prove
W_{\tau}\subseteq E^{\prime}_{\tau}
for all
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
.
Let
W^{(0)}_{\tau}\subseteq W^{(1)}_{\tau}\subseteq\dots
be the smallest sets satisfying:

(1)

If
s
is a leaf and
R\subseteq\{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})\mid W_{\tau}^{(k)}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset\}

then
E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq W_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}^{(k+1)}
.

(2)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and

, then

.

(3)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
W_{(p,r^{x},q)}^{(k)}\cap Z_{r^{x}}\neq\emptyset
, then
(p,r^{x},q)\in W_{(p,s^{y},q)}^{(k+1)}
.

(4)

If
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mu\in W_{(p,r^{x},q)}^{(k)}
and
\nu\in W_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}^{(k)}
, then
\nu+\mu\in W_{(p,r^{x},q)}^{(k+1)}
.

We prove
W^{(k)}_{\tau}\subseteq E^{\prime}_{\tau}
for all
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
by induction on

.

(1)

Let
R\subseteq\{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})\mid W_{\tau}^{(k)}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset\}
.
Since
E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq W^{(k+1)}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}

we need to show the inclusion

.
By induction hypothesis we know that

for all

.
Therefore all

are realizable, which implies
E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq E^{\prime}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

.

(2)

Suppose that
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
W_{(p,s^{y},q)}^{(k)}\cap Z_{s^{y}}\neq\emptyset
.
Take
\kappa\in W_{(p,s^{y},q)}^{(k)}\cap Y_{r^{x}}
.
The goal is to show that
\kappa\in E^{\prime}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.
By induction hypothesis
\kappa\in E^{\prime}_{(p,s^{y},q)}
, i.e.

\kappa
is the effect of a realizable almost placeholder run

\displaystyle\sigma\colon(q_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{1}$}\vss}}}p_{1})

\displaystyle(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})(q_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{2}$}\vss}}}p_{2})\cdots

\displaystyle(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})(q_{m}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{m}$}\vss}}}p_{m+1})

in
(p,s^{y},q)
.
In particular, all
s_{i}^{x_{i}}
are above or equal to
s^{y}
.
Indeed, all such
s_{i}^{x_{i}}
are above
r^{x}
or equal to
r^{x}
since
\kappa\in Y_{r^{x}}
.
Furthermore we claim that
\check{\pi}_{{r}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})=1
:
First all runs

are in
\mathcal{A}_{s}
.
We know
\check{\pi}_{{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

and also
\hat{\pi}_{{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

for all

.

(3)

Suppose that
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
W_{(p,r^{x},q)}^{(k)}\cap Z_{r^{x}}\neq\emptyset
.
The goal is to show that
(p,r^{x},q)\in E^{\prime}_{(p,s^{y},q)}
.
By induction hypothesis we know that there exists
\kappa\in E^{\prime}_{(p,r^{x},q)}\cap Z_{r^{x}}
,
which is the effect of a realizable almost placeholder run
\sigma
in
(p,r^{x},q)
.
In fact
\sigma
is a realizable placeholder run since
\kappa\in Z_{r^{x}}
.
Therefore
(p,r^{x},q)
is realizable, and thus
(p,r^{x},q)
is a realizable almost placeholder run in
(p,s^{y},q)
, with effect
(p,r^{x},q)\in E_{(p,s^{y},q)}^{\prime}
.

(4)

Suppose that
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mu\in W_{(p,r^{x},q)}^{(k)}
and
\nu\in W_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}^{(k)}
.
The goal is to prove
\nu+\mu\in E^{\prime}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.
By induction hypothesis
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mu\in E^{\prime}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
and
\nu\in E^{\prime}_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
,
i.e.
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mu
is the effect of a realizable almost placeholder run
\sigma_{1}
in
(p,r^{x},q)

and
\nu
is the effect of a realizable almost placeholder run
\sigma_{2}
in
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})
.
Let
\sigma
be obtained from
\sigma_{1}
by replacing any occurrence of the placeholder
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})

by
\sigma_{2}
.
Observe that
\sigma
is a realizable almost placeholder run in
(p,r^{x},q)
with effect
\nu+\mu
,
and hence
\nu+\mu\in E^{\prime}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.

For the converse direction, we show for all
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
and all realizable almost placeholder runs
\sigma

that its effect belongs to
W_{\tau}
,
by lexicographic induction, where we first order by the
weight
of
\sigma
and then by the “below” order
on the node descriptions
s^{x}
in
\tau
.
For every realizable almost placeholder run
\sigma
in
\tau
we inductively define a
weight
:
If
\sigma=\sigma_{0}(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})\sigma_{1}\cdots(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})\sigma_{m}

then its weight is
|\sigma_{0}\dots\sigma_{m}|+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\omega_{i}

where
|\sigma_{0}\dots\sigma_{m}|
is the total number of edges used in the runs
\sigma_{i}
,
and
\omega_{i}
is the minimal weight of a realizable placeholder run in
(p_{i},s_{i}^{x_{i}},q_{i})
.

For the induction base we consider a realizable almost placeholder run consisting of a single transition
(p,w,q)
in
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)

for some leaf
s
.
Its effect is contained in
E^{\prime\emptyset}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq W_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}
by (
1
).

For the induction step consider a realizable almost placeholder run

\sigma=\sigma_{0}(p_{1},s_{1}^{x_{1}},q_{1})\sigma_{1}\cdots(p_{m},s_{m}^{x_{m}},q_{m})\sigma_{m}

in
(p,s^{x},q)
,
i.e. (i)

and

, (ii) for

,

is above or equal to

and
(p_{i},s_{i}^{x_{i}},q_{i})
is realizable,
(iii)
\sigma_{i}\colon q_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{i}$}\vss}}}p_{i+1}
is a run in
\mathcal{A}_{s}
for each

and (iv)
\check{\pi}_{{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})=1
.

(1)

If
s^{x}={s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}
then

A saturation procedure

Consider the definition of
W_{\tau}
. In order to argue that an element belongs
to
W_{\tau}
, we need to apply two kinds of steps alternatingly: (i) produce new
elements in
W_{\tau}
using rules (
1
)–(
4
) and (ii) observe that

W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
has become non-empty so as to enable more rules among
(
1
)–(
4
). Here, our key idea is to use grammars to decide if
applying rules (
1
)–(
4
) proves a set
W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
non-empty.
Hence, we assume that for a certain set
R\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{A})
we have already established
that
W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
for every
\tau\in R
. Then, we construct grammars
and decide their emptiness to check if this leads to more
\tau
such that
W_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
is non-empty.

Let us make this formal. Let
R\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{A})
be a subset.
We inductively define the tuple
(W^{R}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})}
, where
W^{R}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}+\mathbb{Z}^{V}
for every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
. It is the smallest tuple such that

(1)

If
s
is a leaf, then
E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\subseteq W^{R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}
.

(2)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,s^{y},q)\in R
, then
W^{R}_{(p,s^{y},q)}\cap Y_{r^{x}}\subseteq W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.

(3)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,r^{x},q)\in R
, then
(p,r^{x},q)\in W^{R}_{(p,s^{y},q)}
.

(4)

If
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
and
\mathbf{v}\in W^{R}_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
, then
\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
.

We now perform the procedure outlined above: We start with
R^{(0)}=\emptyset

and then set
R^{(i+1)}=\{\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})\mid W_{\tau}^{R^{(i)}}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset\}
. Then we clearly have
R^{(0)}\subseteq R^{(1)}\subseteq\cdots
and thus there is some
n
with
R^{(n+1)}=R^{(n)}
.
The following is immediate from the definition of
W_{\tau}
.

Proposition D.3.

For every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
, we have
W^{R^{(n)}}_{\tau}=W_{\tau}
.

Thus, in order to decide
\mathsf{BIREACH}
in
\mathsf{P}
, it suffices to decide,
given a set
R^{(i)}
as above, whether
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
is empty for each

\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
. To do this, we will construct certain grammars for which we
will show that emptiness can be decided in polynomial time. Here, we will use
the fact that each
R^{(i)}
is obtained from the process above: We will assume
that
R\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{A})
is
admissible
, meaning that there exists some

i
with
R=R^{(i)}
.

The grammar construction

Let us now show how to construct a
grammar
G=(N,T,P)
such that
N=\rho(\mathcal{A})
and
L(\tau)=W_{\tau}^{R}
for every

\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
. In our definition of grammars, we require that every
production either has the form (1)
a\to\mathbf{u}
with
a\in N_{0}
and
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{0}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T}
or (2)
a\to b
with
a\in N_{i}
and
b\in N_{j}
with
|i-j|=1
,
i.e.
a
and
b
are nonterminals on neighboring levels. For the construction,
it will be convenient to more generally allow productions
a\to b

where
a\in N_{i}
and
b\in N_{j}
for
i\neq j
. If we have such a grammar,
one can introduce intermediate nonterminals to achieve the more restrictive format.
Moreover, this introduction of intermediate nonterminals preserves bidirectedness.

If we allow these more general productions, we need to specify how they
induce derivation steps. The relation
\,\Rightarrow_{0}\,
is defined as
before. However, once
\,\Rightarrow_{i^{\prime}}\,
is defined for all
i^{\prime}<i
, we have

\mathbf{v}\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
iff there is an
a\in N_{i}
with
\mathbf{v}(a)>0
and a production

a\to a^{\prime}
for some
a^{\prime}\in N_{i^{\prime}}
for some
i^{\prime}<i
, and a
\mathbf{u}\in L(a^{\prime})\cap\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
such that
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}=\mathbf{v}-a+\mathbf{u}
. Here, we had to put
the intersection with
\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
, because if
a^{\prime}\in N_{i^{\prime}}
with
i^{\prime}<i-1
, then
L(a^{\prime})
is not necessarily included in

\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
.

Recall that
\Gamma=(V,I)
. The set
T
will consist of the looped vertices of
\Gamma
, but also
some auxiliary letters defined as follows. We pick an arbitrary linear order
\ll
on
Q
. Then we have the letters

\Theta=\{z_{p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

.
In other words, for any two states in

of

Theorem

6.5
. We construct a valence system
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}

over a graph

as follows. The graph

has vertices

that belong to

and

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q^{\prime})\in R\mid p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in Q\}
. Moreover,

is a clique, and a vertex is looped if and only if it is

. In other words,

another looped vertex. Then

is obtained from

by adding, for any

In addition to these level-
0
productions, we also add the following for every
r^{x}
and
s^{y}
:

(C1)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,s^{y},q)\in R
, then we have a production
(p,r^{x},q)\to(p,s^{y},q)
.

(C2)

If
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,r^{x},q)\in R
, then we have a production
(p,s^{y},q)\to(p,r^{x},q)
.

We prove the following:

Proposition D.4.

Given a graph
\Gamma
in
\mathsf{SC}^{\pm}_{d,\ell}
and a valence system
\mathcal{A}

over
\Gamma
, and an admissible
R\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{A})
, we can
construct in polynomial time a bidirected
k
-grammar
G=(N,T,P)
with
k\leq 2\ell
such
that
N=\rho(\mathcal{A})
and
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
if and only if
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
for each
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
with
\tau\in N_{i}
.

Before we verify
Proposition

D.4
, let us see how it implies
Proposition

7.1
.

Proof of
Proposition

7.1
.

By
Lemma

D.1
, we have to decide
whether
E_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\neq\emptyset
. By

Proposition

D.2
, this is equivalent to

W_{(p,{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}\cap Z_{{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\neq\emptyset
. In order to decide the
latter, we apply the saturation procedure described after

Proposition

D.3
. We start with
R^{(0)}
and then
compute the set
R^{(i+1)}
from
R^{(i)}
. Now

Proposition

D.4
allows us to construct for
R^{(i)}
in
polynomial time a bidirected
k
-grammar
G
with
k\leq 2\ell
such
that
\tau\in R^{(i+1)}
if and only if
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
. Thus,
by invoking the emptiness problem for
G
, we can compute
R^{(i+1)}

from
R^{(i)}
. In the end, we have reachability if and only if

(p,{t}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)\in R^{(n)}
.

We invoke emptiness of bidirected grammars at most polynomially many
times, because the saturation must finish in polynomially many steps:
Each
R^{(i)}
is a subset of
\rho(\mathcal{A})
, which has polynomially many
elements, we must have
R^{(n+1)}=R^{(n)}
for some polynomially
bounded
n
.

Finally, since
k\leq 2\ell
in
Proposition

D.4
, we indeed
invoke emptiness of bidirected grammars only for
k\leq 2\ell
if the
input graphs are drawn from
\mathsf{SC}^{\pm}_{d,\ell}
.
∎

Let us observe that the same proof also yields an exponential space Turing
reduction if the input graphs are drawn from all of
\mathsf{SC}^{\pm}
. Here, it is
important to notice that although this is an exponential space reduction, it
yields
k
-grammars where
k
and the number of productions is still
polynomial. However, the numbers occurring in the productions can be doubly
exponential (and thus use exponentially many bits).

Proposition D.5.

There is an exponential space Turing reduction from
\mathsf{BIREACH}(\mathsf{SC}^{\pm})

to emptiness of bidirected
k
-grammars. Here, the resulting

k
-grammars will have
k
and the number of productions bounded
polynomially in the input. Moreover, the numbers occurring in
productions require at most exponentially many bits.

The rest of this section is devoted to proving
Proposition

D.4
.

Languages generated by the constructed grammar

We prove that
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
if and only
W^{R}_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
.
We prove that
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
if and only if
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
by showing that (i)
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\subseteq L(\tau)
for every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
and
that (ii) for every
\mathbf{u}\in L(\tau)
with
\tau\in N_{i}
, there exists a

\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{i}
such that
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}\in W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
. Here,
\Delta_{i}
is defined as follows. For
i\in[0,k-1]
, we define
\Delta_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}
as the submonoid generated by all
a+{a}^{\dagger}
with
a\in R_{[i+1,k]}
. Moreover,
\Delta_{k}
consists just of
\mathbf{0}
.
Thus, we establish the following two facts.

Proposition D.6.

For every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
, we have
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\subseteq L(\tau)
.

Proposition D.7.

For every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
with
\tau\in N_{i}
and every
\mathbf{u}\in L(\tau)
, there exists a
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{i}
such that
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}\in W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}
.

Observe that
Propositions

D.6
and
D.7
together imply that
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
if and only if
W^{R}_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
.

We begin with
Proposition

D.6
. We show
Proposition

D.6
by proving that for every
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{\tau}

with
\mathbf{u}\in N_{i}
, we have
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
in the grammar. This implies that
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\subseteq L(\tau)
.
We first prove two auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma D.8.

For every
a\in R_{0}
, we have
a+{a}^{\dagger}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{0}\,\mathbf{0}
.

Proof.

Let

Lemma D.9.

For any

and every leaf
s
with
a=(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

and

\mathbf{x}\in\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,q)
, we have
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

.

Proof.

Let
s
be a leaf of
t
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,q)
. In the
construction of the grammar, we compute
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots\mathbf{u}_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{R_{s}\cup L_{s}}
so that
\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,q)=\mathbf{v}+\langle\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{n}\rangle
.
Therefore, we can write

\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{v}+x_{1}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{n}+x^{\prime}_{1}\cdot(-\mathbf{u}_{1})+\cdots+x^{\prime}_{n}\cdot(-\mathbf{u}_{n})

for some
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that we can derive

(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{0}\,\alpha(\mathbf{v})+\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}\cdot\alpha(\mathbf{u}_{j})+x^{\prime}_{j}\cdot\alpha(-\mathbf{u}_{j})

by using each production
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

exactly

-times, then
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

By

for each such

This implies that

.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}(w_{1}\cdots w_{m})
belongs to
\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,q)
. By
Lemma

D.9
, we have
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{0}\,\mathbf{y}
.

Since we have a production
(p_{j},{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q_{j})\to(p_{j},s_{j}^{x_{j}},q_{j})
for each

, we can derive
\mathbf{y}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{0}\,\mathbf{x}
. Hence we have
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Proposition

D.6
.

Proof of
Proposition

D.6
.

As mentioned above, we prove that for every
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
with
\tau\in N_{i}
, we have
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
.

We proceed by induction on the number of rule applications used to
conclude that
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
. Put differently, we proceed by induction on
n
and we assume that
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
if
\mathbf{u}
’s membership in
W^{R}_{\tau}
can be derived in
<n

steps. And we prove that then every
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
, whose membership can be
derived in
n
steps, also satisfies
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
.

The induction base is done in
Lemma

D.10
. So suppose
\mathbf{u}
belongs to
W^{R}_{\tau}
and can be derived in
n
steps. We consider each rule in the definition of
W^{R}_{\tau}
:

(1)

Suppose
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,s^{y},q)\in R
and
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,s^{y},q)}\cap Y_{r^{x}}
. Then since
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,s^{y},q)}
and can be derived in
<n
steps, we know
by induction that
(p,s^{y},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{u}
, where
i^{\prime}
is the level of
s^{y}
.

There exists a production
(p,r^{x},q)\to(p,s^{y},q)

since
(p,s^{y},q)\in R
,. Moreover, the fact that

\mathbf{u}\in Y_{r^{x}}
allows us to conclude

(p,r^{x},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
.

(2)

Suppose
r^{x}
is above
s^{y}
and
(p,r^{x},q)\in R
. Then there is a production
(p,s^{y},q)\to(p,r^{x},q)
and hence we have
(p,s^{y},q)\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,(p,r^{x},q)
, where
i
the level of
s^{y}
.

(3)

Suppose that
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
and
\mathbf{v}\in W^{R}_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
, where the memberships
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
and
\mathbf{v}\in W^{R}_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
can be derived in
<n
steps. We have to show that

(p,r^{x},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u}
, where
i
the level of
r^{x}
.

By induction, we know
(p,r^{x},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}
and that
(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{v}
.
By applying these derivations successively, we obtain
(p,r^{x},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u}
.

This establishes that indeed for every
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
with
\mathbf{u}\in N_{i}
, we have
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
.
This implies the inclusion
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\subseteq L(\tau)
: If
\mathbf{u}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
, then we know
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
. Since furthermore
\mathbf{u}\in Z_{\tau}
, we even have
\mathbf{u}\in L(\tau)
.
∎

Our next step is to prove
Proposition

D.7
. We begin with the case that
\tau=(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)
for some leaf
s
of
t
.

Lemma D.11.

be a leaf of

and

. For every

Proof.

We first observe that if

using only productions

,

and

, then
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in E^{\prime R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)}
.
This follows by induction from

,

and

. For

belongs to

.

\mathbf{u}\in L(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

. Then

with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

contains no

0
terminal symbols, the productions in

Eq.

15
must have been applied pairwise. This means,
we have
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}
for some

\mathbf{d}=(b_{1}+{b_{1}}^{\dagger})+\cdots+(b_{r}+{b_{r}}^{\dagger})
with

. Since

is admissible, this implies that for each

,
we have
W^{R}_{b_{j}}\cap Z_{b_{j}}\neq\emptyset
and also
W^{R}_{{b_{j}}^{\dagger}}\cap Z_{{b_{j}}^{\dagger}}\neq\emptyset
.
Therefore, we can replace in
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}
each

by the effect
\mathbf{u}_{j}
of an
R
-placeholder run in
b_{j}

and we can replace
{b_{j}}^{\dagger}
by
{\mathbf{u}_{j}}^{\dagger}
: Note that
{\mathbf{u}_{j}}^{\dagger}
is the effect of an
R
-placeholder run in
{b_{j}}^{\dagger}
. Let
\mathbf{d}
be the resulting vector in
\mathbb{N}^{\rho(\mathcal{A})}
.
Then clearly
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{0}
. Moreover, we have
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}\in W^{R}_{(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }

.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Proposition

D.7
.

Proof of
Proposition

D.7
.

We prove the statement by induction on
i
:
Lemma

D.11
is the case
i=0
.
Now suppose the statement holds for all
i^{\prime}<i
. To prove the statement for
i
, we apply another induction by the number of derivation steps to derive an element of
L(\tau)
. More precisely, we show that for any
n\geq 1
, if
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
in at most
n
steps, then there exists a
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{i}
such that
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
. Suppose this holds for all step counts
<n
.

Let
\tau\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
in
n
steps with
\tau\in N_{i}
. We distinguish two cases.

(1)

Suppose
n=1
. Then there is a
\tau^{\prime}\in N_{i^{\prime}}
for some
i^{\prime}<i

such that
\tau^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{u}\in L(\tau^{\prime})\cap S_{i-1}
. Since
\mathbf{u}\in L(\tau^{\prime})
, our
induction hypothesis on
i^{\prime}
yields some

\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{i^{\prime}}
such that
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in W^{R}_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap Z_{\tau^{\prime}}
.

Since
\mathbf{u}\in S_{i-1}
, we
also have
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in Y_{\tau}
.

Therefore, it follows from
the definition of
W^{R}_{\tau}
that there is a

\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{i}
with
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
.

(2)

Suppose
n>1
and write
\tau=(p,r^{x},q)
. Then there is a

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
such that
(p,r^{x},q)\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
in
<n

steps and
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})+\mathbf{v}
and some
\mathbf{x}
with

(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,\mathbf{x}
so that
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{v}
.
By our induction hypothesis, we know that there is a

\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{i}
such that
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
. Moreover, there is a

\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in\Delta_{i}
with

\mathbf{x}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in W^{R}_{(p^{\prime},r^{x},q^{\prime})}
. According
to the last rule in the definition of
W^{R}_{\tau}
, this
implies that
\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{x}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in W^{R}_{(p,r^{x},q)}
. In particular, we have

\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}=\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{d}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in W^{R}_{\tau}
. Since
\mathbf{d}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in\Delta_{i}
,
this proves our claim.

∎

Bidirectedness of the constructed grammar

Now we prove that the grammar is indeed bidirected.
The conditions

Items

(1)
,
(3)
and
(5)

are obvious from the construction. Moreover, the condition

Item

(4)
follows from
L(\tau)\neq\emptyset
if and only if
W^{R}_{\tau}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
for every
\tau\in\rho(\mathcal{A})
.
It remains to show the following.

Lemma D.12.

The constructed grammar satisfies
Item

(2)
of the bidirectedness conditions.

Item

(2)
is clear immediately except for

and

. Let us first prove the property for
