and thus
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p_{1},q_{1})
. According to
Lemma

D.9
, we thus have

b=(p_{1},{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q_{1})\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{0}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}={(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y})}^{\dagger}+a.

This concludes the proof for the production
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)\to\alpha(\mathbf{v})
.

For productions
(p,{s}^{\tiny{\hbox to3.65pt{\vbox to3.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.825pt\lower-1.825pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -2.35 -2.35 L 2.35 -2.35 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.14 L -1.04 0 L 1.04 0 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}},q)\to\alpha(\mathbf{u}_{j})
, we can note that if
\mathbf{v}+\langle\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{n}\rangle=\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,q)
, then each
\mathbf{u}_{j}
belongs to
\mathsf{Eff}_{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{s}}(p,p)
and then argue as above.
∎

D.2.
Properties of bidirected grammars

In this subsection, we prove
Lemma

7.3
.
We begin with some simple observations.

Lemma D.13.

If
G=(N,T,P)
is bidirected, and
a\in R_{i}
, then

a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+a+{a}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
i
. For
i=0
, this is one of the
conditions of bidirectedness. For
i>0
and
a\in R_{i}
, we know that
there is some
a^{\prime}\in R_{i-1}
such that there
are productions
a\to a^{\prime}
and
a^{\prime}\to a
. By
induction, we have
a^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i-1}\,a^{\prime}+a^{\prime}+{a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}
. Moreover, since
there is a production
{a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\to{a}^{\dagger}
and
a\in R_{i}
guarantees a
production
a^{\prime}\to a
, we obtain
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+a+{a}^{\dagger}
.
∎

Lemma D.14.

If
G
is
i
-bidirected and
a\in R_{i}
with
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
for some
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
with
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\approx_{a}\mathbf{u}
, then there is a
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{a}
with
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}
.

Proof.

First, we claim that for any
a,b\in R_{i}
with
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
, there
exists a
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{a}
such that
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+b+{b}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
. If
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
, there is a

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
with
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}
such that

a\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}
. Since
G
is
i
-bidirected, we have
b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
for some
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
. By

Lemma

D.13
, we have
b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+b+{b}^{\dagger}
and thus

a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+b+{b}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{v}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}+b+{b}^{\dagger}.

Since
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
, we have
\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{a}
. This proves our claim.

Now consider
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
. Since
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\approx_{a}\mathbf{u}
, we know that
there exist
\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{a}
with
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}
.
By our claim, we know that there is a
\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}\in\Delta_{a}
such that

a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
. Thus, we have

a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

The following lemma tells us that
i
-bidirectedness can be checked as a property
of each derivation step.

Lemma D.15.

If
G
is bidirected, then the following are equivalent:

(1)

G
is
i
-bidirected.

(2)

for
every
a\in R_{i}
and every step
a\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}
with
b\in R_{i}
and

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T}
, there is a
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T}
with

b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Clearly, if
G
is
i
-bidirected, then the second condition is satisfied, so let us show the converse.
First, observe that if the second condition holds, then
\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}
is
symmetric as a relation on
R_{i}
: Indeed, if
a\leadsto b
, then the condition implies that

b\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}a
.

Now suppose
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}
with
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}

and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
. We pick one letter
b\in R_{i}
occurring in
\mathbf{u}
and write
\mathbf{u}=b+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
for some
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}
. In the derivation
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}
, we choose the chain
a_{0},\ldots,a_{n}
of nonterminals that leads to
b
, i.e.
a_{0}=a
,
a_{n}=b
and such that
a_{j+1}
is created by replacing
a_{j}
for
j\in[0,n-1]
. Hence, there are vectors

\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
so that we use the productions
a_{j}\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,a_{j+1}+\mathbf{x}_{j+1}
for
j\in[0,n-1]
to obtain
b
. Then, we have
a_{0}\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,a_{1}+\mathbf{x}_{1}\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,a_{2}+\mathbf{x}_{2}+\mathbf{x}_{1}\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,\cdots\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,a_{n}+\mathbf{x}_{n}+\cdots+\mathbf{x}_{1}
and also
\mathbf{x}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{x}_{n}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v}
.
By the second condition and
Lemma

D.14
, for each
j\in[1,n]
, there exists a
\mathbf{d}_{j}\in\Delta_{a}
such that
a_{j}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a_{j-1}+{\mathbf{x}_{j}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}_{j}
. Putting these together, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}b=a_{n}&\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a_{0}+{\mathbf{x}_{1}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}_{1}+\cdots+{\mathbf{x}_{n}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}_{n}\\
&=a+{\mathbf{x}_{1}}^{\dagger}+\cdots+{\mathbf{x}_{n}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d},\end{split}

where we define
\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{d}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{d}_{n}
.
Since
\mathbf{x}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{x}_{n}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v}
, we therefore have

b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+{(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v})}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
and thus

\mathbf{u}=b+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+{(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v})}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=a+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}^{\dagger}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}.

Finally, since
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}
and thus
L(c)\neq\emptyset
for every
c
occurring in
\mathbf{u}
(and also
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}c
), we know that there is some
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i+1,k]}}
with
\mathbf{u}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{e}
. But then we have
\mathbf{u}+{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{e}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\dagger}
, where
\mathbf{e}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\dagger}\in\Delta_{a}
. Thus, we have
\mathbf{u}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{e}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\dagger}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
.
Now with
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}={\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{e}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
, we clearly have
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
. Thus,
G
is
i
-bidirected.
∎

See
7.3

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
i
, so suppose
G
is
(i-1)
-bidirected.

According to
Lemma

D.15
, it suffices to show that for

a\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}
with
a,b\in R_{i}
and

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T}
, there exists a

\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
with
b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
. If

a\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,b+\mathbf{v}
, then this is due to a production
a\to\tilde{a}

for some
\tilde{a}\in R_{i-1}
, a derivation
\tilde{a}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i-1}\,\tilde{b}+\mathbf{v}
, and a production
\tilde{b}\to b
.
Since
G
is

(i-1)
-bidirected, there exists a
\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\approx_{\tilde{a}}{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}

and a derivation
\tilde{b}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i-1}\,\tilde{a}+\tilde{\mathbf{v}}
. Since

\tilde{a}+\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\approx_{\tilde{a}}\tilde{a}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}
, we know from

Lemma

D.14
that we can derive

\tilde{b}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\tilde{a}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\tilde{\mathbf{d}}
for some

\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in\Delta_{\tilde{a}}
. Moreover, since

\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\in\Delta_{\tilde{a}}
, there is a
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{a}

with
\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i-1}\,\mathbf{d}
. Hence, we have

\tilde{b}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i-1}\,\tilde{a}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
.

Note that since
a,b\in R_{i}
, we also have productions
b\to\tilde{b}

and
\tilde{a}\to a
, so that we also have

b\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,a+{\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
and thus
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}={\mathbf{v}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
is as desired.
∎

D.3.
Expressing emptiness in terms of cosets

Lemma D.16.

If
G=(N,T,P)
is
i
-bidirected, and
a\in R_{i}
and
b\in R_{i}
with
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
. Then there is some
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{a}
such that
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+b+{b}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{d}
.

Proof.

Since
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
, there is some

\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
with
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+\mathbf{u}
. Since

G
is
i
-bidirected, this implies
b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
with

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
. By
Lemma

D.13
, we know that

b\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+b+{b}^{\dagger}
. Hence, we have
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+\mathbf{u}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+b+{b}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{u}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a+\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+b+{b}^{\dagger}
. Now observe that since

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\approx_{a}{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
, we have
\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{a}
.
∎

See
7.7

Proof.

Consider some
b\in R_{i+1}
with
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
. Then there must be an
a^{\prime}\in R_{i}
with
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}a^{\prime}
and a production
a^{\prime}\to b
. By
Lemma

D.13
,
we have
a^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a^{\prime}+a^{\prime}+{a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}
and thus
a^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,a^{\prime}+b+{b}^{\dagger}
. Therefore, we have
b+{b}^{\dagger}\in H_{a}
.
∎

See
7.8

Proof.

Since
G
is bidirected, we know that
D_{a}\subseteq H_{a}
. Thus, the
inclusion “
\supseteq
” is clear. For the the converse, it suffices
to show that for every
b\in R_{i}
with
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
and

b\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
, the vector
-(-b+\mathbf{u})
belongs to the right-hand side.
We write
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{x}
with
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}
and

\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T}
. Since
G
is
i
-bidirected, we know
that
\mathbf{v}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,b+\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}+D_{b}
.
But this implies
-\mathbf{v}+b+\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
belongs to the monoid on the
right-hand side. Moreover, the two elements
-(-b+\mathbf{u})
and
-\mathbf{v}+b+\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
differ in

-(-b+\mathbf{u})-(-\mathbf{v}+b+\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=-\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in-\mathbf{x}-{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger}+D_{b}\subseteq D_{b}

and since
a\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b
and
G
is
i
-bidirected, we also have
b\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}a

and thus
D_{b}=D_{a}
. Thus
-(-b+\mathbf{u})
belongs to the right-hand side.
∎

See
7.9

Proof.

Since (2) is a direct consequence of the definition and previous lemmas, it remains to prove (1).
Suppose
L(a)\neq\emptyset
. Then there is a derivation

a\,\Rightarrow_{i}\,\mathbf{u}_{1}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
with

\mathbf{u}\in S_{i}
. In particular, the first derivation step must be due to
some production
a\to a^{\prime}
with
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in L(a^{\prime})
. This means
a^{\prime}\in R_{i-1}
and thus
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in L_{a^{\prime}}
according to

Theorem

7.4
. Write
\mathbf{u}_{1}=b_{1}+\cdots+b_{n}+\mathbf{v}
, where
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\in R_{i}
and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
.
Since
\mathbf{u}_{1}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}
, we know that for each
j\in[1,n]
, we have
b_{j}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{v}_{j}
for some
\mathbf{v}_{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i,k]}}
such that
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{v}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{v}_{n}+\mathbf{v}
. This means in particular that
\mathbf{v}_{j}\in M(b_{j})
and thus

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{i}\ni\mathbf{u}&=\mathbf{u}_{1}+(-b_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{1})+\cdots+(-b_{n}+\mathbf{v}_{n})\\
&\in L_{a^{\prime}}+\langle-b_{1}+M_{b_{j}}\rangle+\cdots+\langle-b_{n}+M_{b_{n}}\rangle.\end{split}

Moreover, since
b_{1}+\cdots+b_{n}+\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\in L(a^{\prime})
, the nonterminals
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}
satisfy
a^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b_{j}
for
j\in[1,n]
. Thus,
\mathbf{u}
belongs to
K_{a^{\prime}}
.

Conversely, suppose
K_{a^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
for some production
a\to a^{\prime}
.
Then
a^{\prime}\in R_{i-1}
and thus
L_{a^{\prime}}=L(a^{\prime})+D_{a^{\prime}}
according to

Theorem

7.4
. Consider some
\mathbf{u}\in K_{a^{\prime}}
and write

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{v}_{n}
with
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in L_{a^{\prime}}
and

\mathbf{v}_{j}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{j}}(-b_{j}+\mathbf{x}_{j})
for some
b_{j}\in R_{i}
,

a^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\leadsto}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}b_{j}
, and
\mathbf{x}_{j}\in M_{b_{j}}
.

In order to construct a derivation, we have to eliminate those summands

\mathbf{v}_{j}
with
\varepsilon_{j}=1
, because they do not directly correspond
to derivation steps. Hence, we define
\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}

as follows. For
j\in[1,n]
, if
\varepsilon_{j}=0
, then
\hat{b}_{j}=b_{j}

and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{j}=\mathbf{x}_{j}
; if
\varepsilon_{j}=1
, then
\hat{b}_{j}={b_{j}}^{\dagger}
and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{j}={\mathbf{x}_{j}}^{\dagger}
. Then
\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{j}=-\hat{b}_{j}+\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{j}
.
Then each difference
\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-\mathbf{v}_{j}
is either
\mathbf{0}
or

(-{b}^{\dagger}_{j}+{\mathbf{x}}^{\dagger})-(-1)(-b_{j}+\mathbf{x}_{j})=-{b_{j}}^{\dagger}-b_{j}+{\mathbf{x}_{j}}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{x}_{j}
. Therefore, with
\mathbf{b}=b_{1}+{b_{1}}^{\dagger}+\cdots+b_{n}+{b_{n}}^{\dagger}
and

\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{x}_{1}+{\mathbf{x}_{1}}^{\dagger}+\cdots+\mathbf{x}_{n}+{\mathbf{x}_{n}}^{\dagger}
, we have

\mathbf{u}_{1}+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\cdots+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}+\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{v}_{n}+\mathbf{g}.

Since
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in L(a^{\prime})+D_{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{b}\in\Delta_{a^{\prime}}
, we have
\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{b}\in L(a^{\prime})+D_{a^{\prime}}
, so that
Lemma

D.16
implies that we can write
\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{d}
with some
\mathbf{y}\in L(a^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{d}\in\Delta_{a^{\prime}}
. This implies

(18)

\mathbf{y}+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\cdots+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}=\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{v}_{n}+\mathbf{g}+\mathbf{d}.

Now notice that since
\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{d}
, we know that
\mathbf{b}
must occur in
\mathbf{y}
. In particular,
\hat{b}_{1}+\cdots+\hat{b}_{n}
must occur in
\mathbf{y}
.
Hence, we have a
derivation
a^{\prime}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{y}+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\cdots+\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}
. By

Eq.

18
, we have thus derived

\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{v}_{n}+\mathbf{g}+\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{g}+\mathbf{d}
. Since
\mathbf{u}\in S_{i}

and
\mathbf{g}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}\subseteq S_{i}
, it remains to eliminate the
level-
i
nonterminals in
\mathbf{d}
. However, since
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{R_{i}}
, we can pick
for each
f
occurring in
\mathbf{d}
some
\mathbf{e}_{f}\in L(e)
,
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i+1,k]}}
. Then, if
\mathbf{d}=f_{1}+\cdots+f_{r}
, then we have
a\,\mathrel{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}\vss}}}_{i}\,\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{g}+\mathbf{e}_{f_{1}}+\cdots+\mathbf{e}_{f_{r}}\in\mathbb{N}^{N_{[i+1,k]}}+\mathbb{Z}^{T_{[i+1,k]}}
. We therefore have
L(a)\neq\emptyset
.
∎

D.4.
From coset circuits to linear Diophantine equations

See
7.12

Proof.

We begin with (1) and (2). By
Proposition

7.1
, it suffices
to show that emptiness for bidirected
k
-grammars is decidable in

\mathsf{EXP}
if
k
is part of the input, and in
\mathsf{P}
for fixed
k
.

Given a bidirected
k
-grammar, we construct a coset circuit
C
as
described in
Algorithm

1
by alternating emptiness
checks for gates and building new gates. To check gates for
emptiness, recall that the circuit has depth
\leq ck
for some
constant
c\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus, it remains to decide emptiness of a gate
g

in exponential time, resp. in polynomial time for bounded depth
circuits. For each gate
g
in this coset circuit, we compute a
matrix representation for the coset
C(g)
. As argued above, the
resulting matrix
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{Z}^{s\times t}
and vector
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}^{s}
will
satisfy
s,t\leq(r+1)^{ck}
, where
r
is the largest in-degree of a
gate in
C
. The magnitude of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{b}
is at most the magnitude
of the matrices in the leaves of
C
, which means the entries of
\mathbf{A}

and
\mathbf{b}
only require polynomially many bits. Therefore, the size of

\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{b}
is polynomial in
(r+1)^{ck}
. Hence, by

Theorem

3.1
, we can decide emptiness of
C(g)
in time polynomial
in
(r+1)^{ck}
.

For (3), we proceed slightly differently. Since now even
d
is part of the
input, we use
Proposition

D.5
instead of

Proposition

7.1
. Then, the dimensions of all vectors and the
number of productions in the grammar are still polynomial. Therefore,
our coset circuit has linear depth and the matrices labeling its leaves
have entries that require exponentially many bits. The resulting
matrix for each gate therefore has an exponential number of rows and
columns and its entries require at most exponentially many bits. Thus,
we can again apply
Theorem

3.1
to check emptiness of the gate in
exponential time. All together, we obtain an exponential space
algorithm.
∎
